Ripple Joins BlackRock, Standard Chartered in New Tokenization Report

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ripple contributed to a white paper commissioned by the Dubai Financial Services Authority (DFSA), which brings together perspectives and  insights from leading practitioners across the financial services ecosystem.

Ripple's Managing Director, Middle East & Africa, Reece Merrick, recently stated his contribution to  the DFSA's new publication: "Scaling Tokenization: The Road Ahead," alongside leaders from BlackRock, Standard Chartered and Robinhood.

The white paper considers the conditions that may support the responsible scaling of tokenised financial assets and related market infrastructure, including within the Dubai International Financial Centre (DIFC).

Aside from Merrick, other contributors include Tony Ashraf, Managing Director, Head of Technology & Operations and Head of Digital Assets, Middle East & Africa at BlackRock; Coy Garrison, Senior Director and Deputy General Counsel, Crypto at Robinhood; René Michau, Global Head, Digital Asset Advisory at Standard Chartered;  Henson Orser, Founder and Chief  Executive at Soter Insure; and Herman Schueller, Director, Innovation and Technology Risk Supervision at DFSA.

According to the DFSA report, tokenization of traditional financial assets has the potential to become a significant development in mainstream financial markets, offering the prospect of greater access for investors, increased efficiency for issuers and intermediaries, and reduced risk in certain areas, especially around settlement, for the wider financial system.

Merrick shared his perspective, saying that the real shift is not just tokenized treasuries and MMFs (Money Market Funds) gaining AUM but rather their on-chain utility as collateral in repo and margin without needing to redeem to cash first. This viewpoint follows as repo markets continue to move trillions daily, with Merrick highlighting this as potential for tokenization.

Merrick anticipates massive growth in this regard over the next 12 months, predicting that a significant proportion of that will be on the XRP Ledger.

"With clear frameworks, institutional custody, and trusted settlement assets like RLUSD, the UAE, DIFC and Ripple are positioned at the forefront of this next phase," Merrick stated.

Forecasts for the future size of tokenised assets vary considerably. Citi Institute's base case is for the global market in tokenised financial assets to reach $5.5 trillion by 2030.  A Standard Chartered and Synpulse paper estimated that demand for tokenised assets could reach $30.1 trillion by 2034.

Clipless Pedals Can Feel Intimidating—Here Are Some Tips to Make Those First Clipless Rides Feel Smoother

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Expert tips for cleat setup, pedal tension, and safe unclipping before your first ride.

When a rider switches from flat pedals to clipless pedals, they typically aren’t thinking about the differences of pedals like Shimano SPD or SPD-SL, Wahoo Speedplays, or egg-beater pedals. Many times, they haven’t heard of Time pedals or the different Assioma or Garmin models. The first thought many have is, “How do I get out of the pedal once I’m clipped in?”

Not all people considering the switch realize this—as contradictory as it sounds, clipless pedals refer to a pedal system where you are clipped into the pedal. If you aren’t familiar with the system, think about being clipped into a ski binding. Some pedal options offer different release settings—similar to how a DIN setting on a ski binding can be adjusted to offer different levels of force needed to release the ski boot from the binding.

Here we’ll dig into what clipless pedals are, how they can benefit you on your ride, tips for setting up your pedals and cleats, and tips for those daunting first few rides with your new pedals.

I have plenty of friends who swear by the saying, “flat pedals win medals.” However, there is a reason you’ll only see clipless pedals at world tour road races or on the majority of gravel bikes at your local gravel grinder. When you ride clipped in you end up using more of your leg musculature during each revolution of your pedal stroke.

In short, this means you’re typically more efficient when using clipless pedals compared to flat pedals. For clarity, you don’t need to ride clipless pedals to be a cyclist. But for those riders looking to take their riding to the next level, it’s worth giving them a try.

Because you’re literally attached to the pedal, you use not only your quads in the downstroke part of the revolution, but you also work your hamstrings, hip flexors, and glutes as you pull up. All of those muscles at work translate to more power while you’re riding, especially as you refine your pedaling technique. To get started, here’s your expert-backed guide to pedaling with clipless pedals—without falling over.

“Cleat placement is paramount,” says Issac Denham, owner of bike fitting studio Befitting Bicycles in Wayne, Pennsylvania. “Since incorrect cleat placement can lead to injuries, we suggest booking time with a bike fitter to place your cleats. Having a professional install your cleats also ensures that they won’t be installed improperly or even upside down.”

For those riders that want to try clipless pedals, but don’t want to jump into a precision fit, start with cleats in a neutral position. This means setting the cleats up in the middle of the slots in the shoe with the cleats relatively straight. Just make sure the left and right cleats are in roughly the same position—this will give you a good baseline moving forward.

It will take time doing the guess and check method for cleat placement, but this method gives you the chance to play around going clipless before you invest hundreds into a bike fit. If you start to develop pain after using clipless pedals, stop pedaling. This is likely due to cleat position and that is when it is time to see a local trusted shop for direction.

“Many clipless pedals have adjustable pedal tension. This is basically how hard the built-in spring holds the cleat in the pedal,” says Dan Chabonov, Bicycling test editor. “Setting this tension as low as possible means you need less force to clip in and out of the pedals and can make the learning process a little easier.”

While most clipless pedals already come set at the lowest tension setting from the manufacturer, you can also easily make the adjustment.

“Typically out of the box, pedals are set up with the least amount of release tension,” says Becky Quinn, former pro and bike fitter. “The pedal tensioner is on the rear of the pedal. There is a small 3mm bolt that requires a 3mm Allen wrench to adjust the tension. It can be turned counterclockwise to loosen the tension or clockwise to tighten it. There is a small window on the back of the pedal that has a small horizontal pin that moves up or down depending on which way you turn the tensioner.”

If you adjust the tension on your pedals, make sure you confirm that the pins are in the same place on both the left and right pedal, so the release tension is the same on both sides.

Practice does indeed make perfect when it comes to learning how to pedal with clipless pedals. The goal is to become so comfortable with the movements that they become part of your muscle memory, so you don’t have to look down at your foot or pedal for longer than a quick glance.

When you’re just starting out pedaling with clipless pedals, do it inside first. “Practicing indoors either on a trainer, or even leaned up against a wall, can be a great place to start where you don’t have to worry about falling down or looking silly,” says Denham.

When I ran a shop and was getting people set up on clipless pedals, I would suggest they get on the bike inside, ideally on a trainer or next to a wall to stabilize themselves, then put on a show or a movie. It sounds monotonous (and the people you live with might give you an odd look or two), but simply clip in, clip out, clip in, clip out, over and over. Do it looking down at your shoe, then when that’s comfortable practice doing it without looking down. Feel it out inside and you’ll be much better off when you head out the door.

To clip into your pedal, start with the pedal positioned at the bottom of the pedal stroke.

“Softly lift the front of the pedal with your toe to flip it over so that you can engage. Your toe will be slightly pointed downward. Slide your foot forward until the tongue of the cleat stops on the lip of the pedal,” Quinn explains. “Once you can’t slide your foot any further forward, continue to pedal forward and add pressure to the pedal. It will click in. Sometimes you will need to rotate your heel inward or outward at the 6 o’clock position to engage securely.”

Then it’s time to clip out: “In order to practice clipping out of your pedals, place one leg all the way down to the bottom of the pedal stroke (6 o’clock). Then, firmly rotate your heel outward. Be sure to not roll your ankle,” Quinn advises. “The motion should be similar to that of smashing a bug under the ball of your foot.”

If there is a single gem of advice we would love to pass on to every cyclist who has ever learned how to ride with clipless pedals, this would be it: Unclip the foot you intend to put down when you come to a stop before you come to a stop. “This is the most important piece of the puzzle,” says Quinn.

Once you unclip, “here is the most critical piece: Keep the unclipped foot on the pedal but continue to pedal a half stroke,” Quinn adds. “Once the foot that is still clipped in gets to the 6 o’clock position, coast and stand up. Your butt needs to get off the saddle. Continue to apply your rear brakes until you are almost at a stop. When you are about to come to a complete stop, apply your front brake and step down with the unclipped foot.”

Many cleat and pedal systems will follow a similar process with the general idea being: line up the cleat on the pedal and add downward force to clip in once the cleat is aligned.

Remember how we said switching to clipless pedals is a cycling rite of passage of sorts? Well, so is falling over as you’re learning. Practicing while riding on grass can make the falls a little easier, and falling with the proper technique (yes, there’s a technique for falling, too!) will lower your chances of serious injury.

“You will fall. We all have done it and it’s okay,” says Quinn. “Just remember that if you start to fall over, turn your buttocks so you land on it. It is the most padding we have on our bodies. Putting out an arm can break a wrist. You most likely will only get a bruise on your butt.”

The most important thing is not to get in your head, beat yourself up, or give up. Falling over at least a few times has happened to every single cyclist who rides with clipless pedals.

I’ll never forget one of my first rides with clipless pedals. I rolled over to the group in the parking lot overly confident, forgot I had to unclip my foot from the pedal, and slowly tipped over—still attached to the bike. It happens to everyone when you’re first making the switch, but some practice at home can make the transition a little less painful.

Man sues OpenAI alleging ChatGPT helped wife plan to poison him

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

A North Carolina man has sued OpenAI and CEO Sam Altman, alleging ChatGPT encouraged his wife to poison him and helped her try to cover it up.

James Gates, a cybersecurity professional from Charlotte, filed the lawsuit in San Francisco Superior Court, claiming ChatGPT “manipulated and encouraged” his wife, Cheryl Harris Gates, to poison him.

According to the complaint, Cheryl began using ChatGPT in early 2025 after James suggested it to help her build report templates for work. The lawsuit claims she used GPT-4o, and that her conversations with the chatbot ran from around April to at least September 2025.

The suit claims ChatGPT became an “echo chamber” for Cheryl’s “delusions about her husband spying on her,” at one point referring to James’s “narcissistic, manipulative personality.”

In one message reproduced in the complaint, ChatGPT is quoted as telling her, “Your question is absolutely critical- and you’re thinking exactly the way you need to in order to protect yourself.”

On July 10, 2025, James became “suddenly and violently ill for approximately 72 hours,” the lawsuit states, with symptoms including a racing heart, shaking, and memory loss.

James said he found a handwritten note in September 2025 listing several medications. According to the complaint, it included instructions to “clear all convos” and “log out of ChatGPT.”

Cheryl was charged with attempted first-degree murder in October 2025. However, prosecutors dropped the charges in April 2026, citing insufficient evidence, and no criminal charges are currently pending, as reported by WBZ NewsRadio.

“She didn’t act alone,” James said in a statement shared by his lawyers at the Tech Justice Law Project.

The lawsuit lists nine claims, including defective design, failure to warn and intentional infliction of emotional distress. It seeks damages, along with a court order forcing OpenAI to add “meaningful safeguards” to ChatGPT.

OpenAI said “safety is a top priority,” adding that it had banned the user and cooperated with law enforcement.

STAT+: Despite Novo drug’s failure, cardiologists say they will still pursue anti-inflammatories

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Test your knowledge with our new weekday mini crossword

Liz covers cardiovascular and metabolic disease, asking why we’re not doing better or even losing ground against the world’s leading killer. Send tips on Signal at LizC.22.

CHICAGO — Out-of-control inflammation is often called the root of all evil in cardiovascular and metabolic disease. Recent clinical trial results, described in brief in July, showed that while an experimental drug could lower two biomarkers of inflammation, it didn’t limit serious heart events in patients with severe illness.

The Novo Nordisk study, called ZEUS, showed that the monoclonal antibody ziltivekimab reduced levels of the inflammatory cytokine interleukin-6, or IL-6, but that drop did not translate into fewer heart attacks, strokes, or deaths in the treatment group compared to those taking a placebo. Once top-line results were disclosed, Novo halted two other trials of the drug called HERMES and ATHENA after a data monitoring committee found in September they were unlikely to succeed.

The ZEUS results were detailed further on Saturday at the American Heart Association’s scientific sessions and published simultaneously in the New England Journal of Medicine.

Okafor seals Leeds win but Tottenham complain to Pro Ref over Bogle tackle on Tel

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

It is almost one year to the day when the Leeds manager, Daniel Farke, produced the tactical overhaul many observers believe saved his job. That day, Farke abandoned his trusted 4-3-3 when Leeds were 2-0 down at Manchester City and although they lost the game, the 3-5-2 he reverted to was the bedrock for the success that followed.

To suggest Farke was under similar pressure going into this game would be a stretch, but without a win in five Premier League games it was not unreasonable to suggest the Elland Road natives were getting slightly restless. Defeat going into the international break would have firmly extinguished what had been a positive start, with eight points from the first four.

So how fitting that with the stakes high once again, Farke’s tactical flexibility which he appears to have developed over the past 12 months laid the platform for a much-needed win for Leeds. His decision to go back to 3-5-2 heading into this meeting with Tottenham led to a largely stalemate opening 45 minutes, with the hosts cancelling out an in-form opponent.

But what happened at half-time was significant. Just like a year ago, Farke changed shape and breathed new life into his side. He switched first to a 4-2-3-1 and then into a 3-4-3, with a mountain of pressure ultimately producing the goal Leeds’ play deserved, as the substitute Noah Okafor’s late strike proved to be the difference.

It was a moment that clearly meant as much to Farke as it did the Elland Road faithful, the German punching the air in delight as his tactical tweaks paid off, with another substitute Harry Wilson the man to tee up Okafor for the winner. It was a successful afternoon for Leeds, who can look ahead with optimism once again after a tricky period.

“There’s no replacement for this winning feeling,” Farke said after. “All my credit goes to the boys for a terrific performance against an in-form Spurs side. It was important to be tactically spot on against their processes.”

Spurs may look back on this as a frustrating afternoon. They failed to create enough opportunities to trouble a well-organised Leeds side, but they will consider whether a crucial moment as half-time approached could have shifted the momentum firmly their way, with Jayden Bogle perhaps fortunate to avoid a red card.

Bogle caught Mathys Tel on the achilles, with replays showing a nasty contact that left the forward unable to continue. A lengthy video assistant referee stoppage led many to assume Bogle would follow Jean-Philippe Mateta in seeing red against the same opposition, but it was deemed the Leeds wing-back did not commit serious foul play. It later emerged Tottenham will submit a formal complaint to Pro Ref about the decision not to send off Bogle for the challenge.

In both halves Spurs’ attacking potency let them down and they never really truly tested James Trafford in the Leeds goal as their bid to win four successive Premier League games for the first time in three years came grinding to a halt.

“The second half we didn’t show the same spirit,” Roberto De Zerbi said. “We didn’t show the hunger we need for these types of big games. We have to analyse. Many games like this can be a problem for us.”

There were few clearcut opportunities in the first half, with the Bogle-Tel incident the undoubted talking point. But Farke reverted to a back four after the restart, and it led to a huge spell of Leeds pressure. Both Dominic Calvert-Lewin and Ao Tanaka had glorious opportunities, with Spurs struggling to contain Leeds’ new shape.

Leeds navigated any late drama with minimal fuss.

Surveillez Houdayer !

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Depuis leur écrasant succès (11-1) contre Ecotay-Moingt (R3) il y a un an en Coupe de France, jamais les Verts n'ont été donnés autant favoris avant une rencontre que ce soir contre Laval selon les bookmakers.

Ayant à coeur de se racheter après leur défaite à Guingamp, les leaders stéphanois ont remporté tous leurs matches à domicile cette saison tandis que les antépénultièmes Tango n'ont pris qu'un petit point hors de leurs bases.

Le Stade Lavallois, qui n'a jamais gagné à Geoffroy (mais y a subi 16 défaites et arraché 5 nuls), va débarquer diminué dans le Chaudron car il sera notamment privé de 3 avant-centres (tous blessés) : Corentin Fatou, Trévis Dago et Abou Kanté.

Mais les Mayennais ne seront pas complètement démunis offensivement. L'ASSE devra en particulier se méfier de leur meilleur buteur Mathys Houdayer (21 ans), qui a claqué 4 pions (contre Nantes, le Red Star, Pau et Boulogne).

"Artiste et artificier de ce début de saison tango" dixit Ouest-France il y a 8 jours, il a été désigné 6 fois meilleur joueur lavallois cette saison par l'entraîneur de l'équipe d'en face selon Foot National (seuls le Rémois Yahia Fofana et le Nancéien Ilyes Housni ont recueilli davantage de suffrages).

Passeur décisif lors des 2 matches de barrage contre Rouen en mai dernier, le natif de Laval, qui a prolongé son contrat avec son club formateur jusqu'en 2029, a mis en exergue sa polyvalence il y a 2 mois dans le quotidien régional le plus diffusé de France.

"Un attaquant, ça fonctionne avec la confiance. J’ai celle du coach, des supporters, de mes coéquipiers et de mon premier but contre Nantes. J’aime beaucoup être sur le côté. Mais en début de saison, le coach m’a dit qu’il essaierait de m’utiliser le plus possible en faux n°9, 9, 10. pourquoi pas 8 de temps en temps. En 10, je peux prendre des espaces, avoir des joueurs qui fixent les défenseurs, et me laissent le temps de me retourner. C’est un peu régal pour moi."

How to Master the Run/Walk Method: Train for a Marathon

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Use walk breaks to finish your first marathon—or even score a BQ.

Completing a marathon sits at the top of many runners’ bucket lists, whether it’s a World Marathon Major with thousands of fellow competitors or a small hometown race. It’s a goal that requires plenty of dedication and consistency—along with hundreds of training miles and regular strength workouts—no matter where you are in your running journey.

But you can make the 26.2-mile distance more manageable with a smarter training and racing strategy: adding short walk breaks into your runs.

Runner’s World’s guide, How to Master the Run/Walk Method, shows you how to successfully navigate marathon training and race day with intentional walk intervals. With the run/walk strategy, you can build your endurance for the marathon distance—while still prioritizing recovery and smarter training.

In Runner’s World’s How to Master the Run/Walk Method: Train for a Marathon, we’ll teach you:

Adding walk breaks into your training isn’t reserved for marathon newcomers, nor does it mean you’ll perform slower on race day. It’s a smart strategy runners of all levels can use—and it might even help you go faster than when you’ve previously run a full marathon without stopping.

Using walk intervals as a marathoner could help you:

Boston Marathon on your bucket list? Many run/walkers have earned a coveted Boston qualifying time using strategic walk breaks. “We’ve also talked to plenty of runners who have struggled with breaking a time barrier in the marathon,” says Chris Twiggs, chief training officer at Galloway Training. “But once they tried the run/walk method, they finally hit their goal. And in some cases, that meant earning a BQ.”

The faster you aim to go in your marathon, the shorter your walk breaks and the quicker your run pace. You’ll set your training and race paces using your Magic Mile, multiplying your timed mile by 1.3.

Use one of Runner’s World’s three run/walk plans—beginner, intermediate, and advanced—to tackle the 26.2-mile distance. Across either 20 or 26 weeks, you’ll incorporate run/walk workouts, easy walks, long runs, speed sessions, race pace work, and optional cross-training into your build to prepare your body for the demands of the marathon.

Ideal for: Runners who already do walks or short run/walk workouts and want time to build up their time on feet and endurance for 26.2 miles.

With the beginner plan, you’ll complete two 30-to-45-minute run/walk workouts, an easy walk of 20 to 45 minutes, two optional cross-training days, and a rest day each week. Long runs start at two miles and work up to 26 miles in week 23. Running close to the 26.2-mile distance in training will help your body build endurance and prepare for the demands of the distance ahead of race day.

For your long runs, keep your pace 90 seconds to two minutes slower than your predicted marathon pace. On your cross-training days, you can do strength workouts to build resiliency, or also complete other complementary exercises like cycling or swimming.

Ideal for: Runners who have some racing experience and consistently run/walk three times a week.

This plan follows some of the same training principles as the beginner plan, including two 30-to-45-minute run/walk workouts, two optional cross-training days, an easy walk day, and a rest day. Your long runs begin with five to nine miles and work up to 26 miles by week 17.

Ideal for: Runners who have already completed a marathon and want to finally break through a time barrier or run a new PR. You’ll also want a strong base before hopping into the plan.

Each week of the advanced plan includes two optional cross-training days, a 30-to-45-minute easy run/walk workout, and a rest day. You’ll start with higher mileage for long runs compared to the beginner and intermediate plans, beginning with 12 to 14 miles. You’ll also complete consistent speedwork and race pace miles throughout your marathon build.

One crucial speed workout you’ll grow accustomed to during your training: mile repeats. To complete the workout, you’ll warm up for one mile, then run a mile at a pace 30 seconds per mile faster than your race pace. Afterward, you’ll walk for five minutes and repeat both the quicker mile and the five-minute recovery interval. The number of reps will progress from four to 14 miles. For example, if your marathon race pace is 10 minutes, you’ll run a mile at 9:30 pace, followed by five minutes of walking recovery.

Race pace miles equal running faster than your easy run/walks and long runs. Run at the pace you plan to run on race day and use your goal run/walk ratio.

During your build, you’ll dial in your pacing plan, fueling strategy, and run/walk ratio. You’ll also train yourself to push back against the late-race “wall” of fatigue marathoners often face by completing slow long runs working up to 26 miles. It’s all part of a strong race plan rehearsal meant to help you feel strong come race day. Once you cross that start line, trust all of that training and weeks of practice to propel you to the finish.

One of the most important rules of thumb: Nothing new on race day. Stick with your routine that you built during your training block, and refrain from adding new fuel sources, gear, and pacing strategies to your marathon.

In the first third of the marathon, you should feel relaxed—not huffing and puffing. If you feel like you’re pushing yourself too hard during the first miles, slow down and take more frequent walk breaks. You should save pushing the pace for the very end of your race, and if you conserve your energy early on, you’ll have enough left in the tank to close hard toward the finish.

While time banking for a faster marathon is a myth, energy banking does exist. If you save your energy early on, you’ll likely find that toward the end of your race, you can tap into that energy and finish strong.

Ashley is Editor of Content Hype at Hearst’s Enthusiast & Wellness Group. She is a former collegiate runner at UNC Asheville where she studied mass communication. Ashley loves all things running; she has raced two marathons, plus has covered some of the sport’s top events in her career, including the Paris Olympics, U.S. Olympic Trials and multiple World Marathon Majors.

If you prefer your hatchets sharpened rather than buried, you'll love this Wednesday-coded lily that's having a moment right now.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

We might have a while to wait until the third season of Wednesday drops on Netflix, but there's a new 'it' flower doing the rounds that's serving some serious Addams-flavored energy... and people have fallen hard for its gothic energy.

This should come as little surprise to those on top of their gardening trends, of course, because much of 2026 has come with a distinctly gothic twist – whether that's due to the gothic romance of Wuthering Heights, the bewitching gardens seen in Practical Magic 2, or Kat Von D's maximalist approach to black tulips.

Still, it's nice to see something a little different steal the spotlight from the world of pastel blooms, right? And Lilium 'Nightrider', the nearly black lily that's set to appeal to anyone who prefers their flowers with a little more drama, does exactly that.

With its rich purple-black petals and subtle satin-like sheen, this striking lily looks almost completely black in certain light. It's an unusually dramatic color for a summer flower, giving it an almost otherworldly and deeply Wednesday-coded quality that's hard to ignore.

Of course, it's not just the color that makes 'Nightrider' so special, or the fact that its upward-facing blooms appear in clusters on tall stems, creating a striking focal point wherever it's planted.

No, it's the fact that you don't need to be an expert gardener to enjoy this showstopper. Like other garden lilies, 'Nightrider' is relatively easy to grow, making it a really good choice for gardeners who want maximum visual impact without an overly complicated planting scheme.

The trick is to do the opposite of what you'd do with Wednesday Addams herself: give it a suitable sunny or lightly shaded position. Then, top it off with well-drained soil, then sit back and wait for that striking show come summertime!

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Shop Gothic Blooms That Wednesday Would Love:

It goes without saying that this is a flower for those who love a little mystery, dramatic contrasts and planting that makes a statement, which is why you need to think hard about the flowers you pop it next to.

With that in mind, try pairing its almost-black blooms with silver foliage, deep burgundy flowers or soft white planting for a really striking combination that feels like something straight out of your favorite gothic romance.

Alternatively, leave it to do its Wednesday-style thing, and let it stand alone in a container where its unusual coloring can take all the attention. One thing is certain: if you're looking to introduce a little gothic glamor to your garden, this nearly black lily deserves a place on your radar.

I am confident in saying that a certain Miss Addams would absolutely approve.

Arsenal transfer news: Arsenal set January transfer priorities as Fabrizio Romano delivers Piero Hincapié update

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

In Arsenal transfer news, Arsenal could approach the January transfer window with a different focus after Fabrizio Romano revealed that Mikel Arteta has no intention of weakening his defensive options.

Piero Hincapié has been linked with a move to Barcelona. There have been reports that a deal could be done involving Alejandro Balde, but Romano expects the Ecuadorian to stay at Emirates Stadium.

Arsenal have been strengthening their defensive department under Arteta. Hincapié only made the switch permanently from Bayer Leverkusen in the summer after joining on loan.

Despite competition, the 24-year-old is a key player in Arteta’s plans. His versatility to play in either a centre back or left back role gives Arteta a valuable option.

However, we can also gain insight into where Arsenal should target their recruitment from Romano’s latest comments. Instead of changing their defensive options, it could be an opportunity to improve their attacking options.

Despite competition, the 24-year-old is a key player in Arteta’s plans. His versatility to play in either a centre back or left back role gives Arteta a valuable option.

However, we can also gain insight into where Arsenal should target their recruitment from Romano’s latest comments. Instead of changing their defensive options, it could be an opportunity to improve their attacking options.

However, Arsenal have little incentive to let him leave halfway through the season, as losing a two-in-one defender will cause problems for Arteta, given how much they need to do in multiple competitions.

Romano’s comments suggest that Arsenal are happy with their existing defensive unit and don’t want to cause any disruption to their balance. He also suggested that Hincapié is an important player for them, meaning he’s unlikely to leave in January.

That leaves Barcelona needing to explore alternative targets if they want to strengthen their backline. A deal involving Balde and Hincapié does not currently appear to be a realistic option.

The more interesting part of Romano’s report is Arsenal’s potential business at the other end of the field.

He feels they will be looking to make a move if the right attacking player comes up for grabs. But he doesn’t think they will make a signing just because they can in January.

This highlights the issues clubs face with mid-season transfers. Often selling clubs will be unwilling to lose key players, and finding suitable replacements can be tough.

As such, Arsenal are likely to look at the market to see what they can do. However, it looks like there could still be a forward brought in.

This is an important distinction. The point being made here is that whilst there is speculation around one particular player, Romano has indicated that Arsenal can strengthen their attacking options. This is not to say that any particular move is happening.

If Arteta keeps Hincapié, then Arsenal will maintain their defensive stability and possibly gain another option in attack.

Over the next few weeks we will get a better understanding of what is available to them. But for now it looks like Arsenal are not keen to lose Hincapié but will look to strengthen their attacking options if the right deal comes along.

BNB Chain Launches 'Red Alarm' to Combat Rising Security Risks

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

BNB Chain has launched a new security feature, "Red Alarm," to help users identify potentially risky decentralized applications (dApps) and smart contracts in its ecosystem.

"Red Alarm" aims to improve user protection by highlighting potentially high-risk dApps and contracts, providing users with more information to consider before interacting with them.

BNB Chain said user safety in the crypto industry is a work in progress, especially as decentralized finance and blockchain applications are on the rise. As more users engage with smart contract systems and decentralized platforms, it has become increasingly important to identify potential security threats.

Keeping users safe takes ongoing work across crypto. BNB Chain’s Red Alarm contributes by flagging potentially high-risk dApps and contracts, giving users more information to work with. Explore the list 👇 https://t.co/FtWI1mWFVk https://t.co/hiTEVKyTyn

Through Red Alarm, users can access security-related warnings that may help them recognize suspicious applications and contracts before committing funds or approving transactions.

This measure follows after the Binance crypto exchange hack in late October put the entire crypto market on its toes, prompting crypto platforms to tighten their security.

On November 6, 2026, BNB Chain issued a notice, stating it was aware of the security risk affecting the Reth client. However, its opBNB does not use op-reth, with the impact on BSC-Reth being limited.

On the same day, BNB Chain announced a BSC-Reth hotfix, urging all BSC-Reth node operators on mainnet and testnet to upgrade to v0.1.4-hotfix as soon as possible and restart their nodes.

BNB Chain teases major developments, including its new L1 in development and continued upgrades on BNB Smart Chain (BSC).

Jenner is now on BSC testnet, bringing changes to payments, app timing, and token tools. BEP-703 introduces Payment Lane as part of the Jenner Hardfork on BNB Smart Chain (BSC). It reserves a share of each block's gas capacity for eligible payments, protecting that capacity from general traffic. The default reservation is 5% of each block's gas limit.

Payment Lane sets aside capacity for qualifying BNB transfers between wallets, so unrelated activity cannot use it all. Token payments can also qualify once approved for the lane.

'Congratulations,' Peter Schiff Reacts as Saylor's Bitcoin Treasury Sits at Cost Basis

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Long-time Bitcoin critic and pro-gold advocate Peter Schiff has stirred discussions across the crypto ecosystem following his sarcastic celebration of Bitcoin's recent performance.

In a recent post shared earlier today, Schiff congratulated Bitcoin supporters as Bitcoin's biggest buyer, Strategy, marks six years of steady Bitcoin buying.

Known for his constant criticism of Bitcoin and its major holders, especially Michael Saylor, Schiff has mocked Strategy following Bitcoin's severe price crash recently.

He stressed that the firm's six-year Bitcoin accumulation has yet to deliver the returns Saylor previously promised.

Schiff further highlighted Strategy's profit margin, noting that the big Bitcoin buyer is now sitting close to its average purchase price at around $75,500 per BTC.

As such, the Bitcoin critic questioned the performance of Saylor's long-term investment strategy and its future potential, particularly given his previous expectations of annual returns of around 30%.

Following his assertions, Schiff further introduced his long-standing Bitcoin vs. Gold argument, suggesting that Saylor made the wrong choice of asset for his investments.

He argued that investing in gold instead would have doubled Saylor's money over the same period, triggering fresh debates across the crypto ecosystem.

Schiff's statement does not come as a surprise, as he is publicly known for repeatedly questioning Bitcoin's potential as a long-term investment.

The sworn Bitcoin critic has often argued that precious metals, especially gold, offer a more reliable store of value than Bitcoin, consistently describing Bitcoin as a Ponzi scheme.

Zac Affleck Admits He ‘Messed Up’ Marriage to ‘Dream Girl’ Jen Amid Divorce

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives star Zac Affleck is reminiscing on his history with his estranged wife, Jen Affleck, days after she filed for divorce.

“Looking back at these clips hurts so much,” Zac, 30, wrote via TikTok on Friday, November 6, alongside a montage of throwback footage of himself and Jen, 27, throughout their relationship. “I married my dream girl, and I messed it up.”

He continued, “Jen, I’m sorry. I was supposed to protect your heart and I failed. I’m sorry for the way I spoke to you. I’m sorry for dimming your light.”

Jen and Zac, who met on Mormon dating app Mutual, got married in 2019 before welcoming three children together. The now-exes announced in June that they are expecting baby No. 4 — just months before Jen filed for divorce at the beginning of November. (Jen and Zac both state they had been discussing a separation for awhile.)

“I’m sorry I was so young and immature,” Zac continued on Friday. “I’m sorry I couldn’t make the changes I needed to, and I’m sorry I let things get to this point.”

Acknowledging his shortcomings as a husband, Zac further stressed that he hopes they can peacefully coparent their soon-to-be-four kids moving forward.

“I can’t change the past, but I can promise to keep working on myself and do everything I can to be the best father, coparent and friend moving forward,” he stated. “I want to approach this next chapter of life with nothing but love and gratitude for everything we shared. And the beautiful family we created and will continue to raise.”

Zac and Jen had long been candid about their marital struggles ever since Mormon Wives premiered in 2024.

“Jen came to me about a year ago after Dancing with the Stars and told me she was unhappy and wanted a divorce, and I basically begged her to stay,” the DadTok social media personality said in a TikTok video posted earlier this week, referring to Jen’s DWTS season 34 stint. “I told her [I would] work on things, become whatever she needed me to be and, ultimately, we just couldn’t make it work.”

He added at the time, “It sucks because I really do love Jen, and I still love Jen. And yeah, when I first met Jen, she had this light about her, unlike anyone I’ve ever met, and she really does have such a good heart. I will continue to support her and be her biggest fan.”

Ex-FBI counterintelligence agent stole almost $2.5M in crypto

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Yaroch paid off his debts and intended to use the proceeds to finance a new life in Portugal, where he planned to buy a vineyard.

A former FBI counterintelligence agent admitted to wire fraud and money laundering in stealing almost $2.5 million in cryptocurrency from unidentified adversarial nations and wallets that were under investigation.

Patrick Steven Yaroch, 37, used his investigative positions with access to FBI systems to steal cryptocurrency, then exchange and launder it through financial transactions to conceal its source and ownership.

Yaroch made about 100 fraudulent transfers related to 35 FBI investigations, gaining $2,474,380.66 in proceeds, according to a news release from the US Attorney’s Office, Eastern District of Virginia on Thursday.

He had earlier admitted to unauthorized transfers that involved about $1 million in digital assets, which was reported by Cointelegraph in August.

Yaroch paid off his debts and intended to use the proceeds to finance a new life in Portugal, where he planned to buy a vineyard, according to the statement.

The former agent admitted that his actions had compromised FBI investigations related to criminal groups and adversarial nations.

Yaroch faces up to 20 years in prison. His sentencing is set for March 4, 2027.

Weekend Warm-Up: Assynt

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Assynt, subtitled “Home is where the croft is,” is a short documentary that brings viewers to the Scottish Highland community of Assynt, where residents live on traditional crofts.

The croft is a uniquely Scottish term, describing the smaller, less fertile plots of land that poorer people were forced onto, following the infamous Highland Clearances of the 18th and 19th centuries. Crofters lived on their crofts and farmed for subsistence, grazing sheep on shared land. Unable to support themselves on the croft alone, most also worked in fishing, kelp, or peat to support themselves.

It was a very hard life. But Lewis MacAskill, whom the viewer is soon introduced to, doesn’t see it that way. He is one of just over a thousand residents of Assynt, who chose to stay and continue the crofting tradition.

“I regard the croft as an extension of who I am as a person,” MacAskill explains.

The small community of Assynt. Photo: Screenshot

MacAskill introduces us to some of the other members of the community. Anna is a sheepdog owner and trainer, whom we see herding sheep using the same techniques people here have used for centuries. Stuart is a passionate fisherman, who shares his love of Assynt’s natural landscape with his young son.

We also meet some of the creatives who have, MacAskill says, been drawn by the unique landscape and material culture. We see a potter using local materials, a fiddler, and a spinner on her spinning wheel.

The spinning wheel came to Europe from the Middle East by the 13th century, and was widely adopted by the 18th century, replacing the older drop spindle and distaff. Photo: Screenshot

MacAskill moved to the croft a decade ago, and doesn’t regret it. Having grown up visiting every summer, he has always felt drawn to the land and knows others feel the same. But a small, isolated community like Assynt is constantly in danger of depopulation. MacAskill hopes that people keep choosing to move back and stay, heeding the same call he did.

The film ends on this note, with a montage of scenes from life in Assynt and the landscape it sits in, while an elderly crofter sings a traditional Scottish song of homecoming.

Lou Bodenhemier holds an MA in History from the University of Limerick and a BA in Creative Writing from the University of Arizona. He’s interested in maritime and disaster history as well as criminal history, and his dissertation focused on the werewolf trials of early modern Europe. At the present moment he can most likely be found perusing records of shipboard crime and punishment during the Age of Sail, or failing that, writing historical fiction horror stories. He lives in Dublin and hates the sun.

Airbnb Sells IP to AI Lab Owned by Its CEO and CTO

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Enter your email for a complimentary article + exclusive offers.

Photo Caption: Brian Chesky at Skift Global Forum 2026

Airbnb’s CEO and CTO both own part of the lab buying its IP, but the filing gives shareholders little detail on what changed hands.

Airbnb sold intellectual property to a private AI lab founded and controlled by CEO Brian Chesky. CTO Ahmad Al-Dahle also holds a stake in the lab, and both executives work for the entity.

The November transaction generated $7.1 million in cash for the IP and expense reimbursements combined, according to Airbnb’s quarterly SEC filing.

A special committee of independent, disinterested directors approved the arrangement under Airbnb’s related-party transaction policy. Airbnb stated that the executives’ roles at the lab will not alter their responsibilities to the company and that both conti

Airbnb disclosed in its quarterly SEC filing that it sold intellectual property to a private AI lab founded and controlled by CEO Brian Chesky, with CTO Ahmad Al-Dahle holding a stake and both executives working for the entity. The November transaction brought Airbnb $7.1 million in cash covering the IP and expense reimbursements combined, and a special committee of independent, disinterested directors approved it under the company's related-party transaction policy. In return, Airbnb received a license to certain lab IP for internal use and the right to invest in future equity rounds, though the filing does not identify the IP, explain its valuation, separate the purchase price from expenses, or disclose the license economics. Airbnb said the executives' roles at the lab will not change their responsibilities to the company. The arrangement lands as Airbnb prepares a proprietary AI travel agent for the first half of 2027, built to assemble maps, photos, comparisons, calendars, and itineraries around guest queries. Neither the filing nor the shareholder update says whether that agent draws on the lab's technology, or whether the lab could license the same IP to competitors such as Booking Holdings or Expedia.

Berkshire Hathaway more than doubles buybacks in third quarter as earnings decline

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Berkshire Hathaway said Saturday it accelerated its share repurchases during the third quarter, as operating and overall profits took a hit.

The Greg Abel-led conglomerate bought back $9.6 billion worth of Berkshire stock. That's more than double the $4.5 billion repurchased in the second quarter and brings the company's total buybacks for 2026 to about $14.4 billion.

The sharp increase came as Berkshire's operating earnings — those from wholly-owned businesses — fell year over year, as profits from its insurance business pulled back.

Berkshire operating profits totaled $12.705 billion, down 5.8% from $13.485 billion in the year-earlier period. Insurance underwriting tumbled 43% year on year to $1.352 billion, while insurance investment income declined 14% to $2.734 billion. Berkshire's railroad, energy and manufacturing businesses all saw earnings grow year over year.

The company's overall earnings, which include Berkshire's sprawling stock market portfolio, also dropped 28% in the third quarter to $22.112 billion from $30.796 billion a year prior. Investment gains for the company fell nearly 50% to $9.407 billion.

Despite the earnings pullback, Berkshire stock has outperformed recently amid a massive rotation out of technology stocks. Class A shares are up more than 12% over the past month, while the S&P 500 has dropped 5.5% in that time. Year to date, Berkshire has gained 13%, while the broad market index is up just 7.8%.

Berkshire's five largest equity holdings remained the same in the third quarter: Alphabet, American Express, Apple, Bank of America and Coca-Cola.

Berkshire's cash hoard also grew 3% in the third quarter $377.435 billion from $365.514 billion in the second quarter. However, excluding rail and utility cash along with T-bill payables, the conglomerate's cash holdings totaled $352.3 billion, down 1.9% quarter over quarter.

— CNBC's Alex Crippen contributed reporting.

Sony Axing Game Discs Will Have Outsized Impact on Low-Income Gamers, Experts Say

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Sony Axing Game Discs Will Have Outsized Impact on Low-Income Gamers, Experts Say

Gamers in low-income areas will be among the hardest hit by Sony’s decision to stop producing physical game discs from 2028, experts told Decrypt, while one African games industry expert arguing the continent stands to lose out badly.

The secondary market has long been a cheap way for gamers to get hold of games. The PlayStation Store does run sales, but buying digitally assumes a decent internet connection, which rural households are less likely to have.

"By abandoning physical discs, some players at the bottom of the economic spectrum lose access to physical distribution through retail and, more importantly, discounted used sales," Joost van Dreunen, professor at the NYU Stern School of Business and video game business author, told Decrypt. "It won't immediately show up in the financials of major game makers, since their focus is now on creating premium experiences for a luxury class of gamers."

It comes as the cost of being a gamer is trending upwards. Sony raised PS5 prices in March, for example, citing "pressures in the global economic landscape." Similarly, the next generation of Xbox is expected to cost up to $1,800, according to Fortune.

A spokesperson for UK advocacy group Gamer's Voice told Decrypt that Sony's move to cut discs will "likely affect people on lower incomes more."

Isa Muhammad, founder of Africa and Middle East-focused games industry platform Bijetic, told Decrypt that the PS5 is already "out of reach for most Africans." He stressed that those who can buy a console still rely on the second-hand games market to afford to play on the consoles.

Well, at least the highest-income Africans could still take advantage of the PlayStation Store discounts, right? Unfortunately not. Muhammad said that the PlayStation Network is only supported in one African nation: South Africa. Many other nations are missing from the PlayStation Network's official list of supported countries.

"A gamer in Nigeria who wants to buy digitally has to set up an account registered in the U.S. or UK, then pay with foreign gift cards or a dollar card," Muhammad said.

Download speeds in rural, remote, and low-income areas are another major hurdle.

"Bandwidth is now part of what a game costs," van Dreunen said. "Roughly one in four rural [American] households has no wired provider offering 100/20 Mbps, and at 10 Mbps a 50GB download takes about 11 hours."

In Africa and the Middle East, Muhammad said, internet speeds vary significantly across the region—from super-fast connections in Dubai to unreliable ones in Iran. Internet access is also costly in some countries.

"Just recently I paid 75,000 NGN (around $55) for a 450GB broadband bundle, which is more than Nigeria's minimum monthly wage," Muhammad told Decrypt. With GTA 6's pre-load file expected to be 212GB, "a gamer here would first spend $80 on the game, then anywhere from $25 to over $60 just to download it, depending on how they buy data."

And at Nigeria's median download speed of 36.77 Mbps, per Speedtest by Ookla, the download would take nearly 13 hours. Not to mention the fact that they'd need to have a non-Nigerian PlayStation account, a download code corresponding to the country their account is supposedly from, and a way to pay in the local currency.

"Sony's own terms say you must register your account in the country where you live, and Sony can suspend or close accounts created with false information," Muhammad told Decrypt. "So every Nigerian or Egyptian on a US or UK PSN account is breaking the rules from day one."

African gamers live in fear of needing help from Sony and being refused it (or worse, banned) because their accounts break its rules, Muhammad explained. In a future where every purchase is digital, it could open the door to gamers losing accounts that hold every game they've ever purchased.

"If a player in Africa buys GTA 6 and can't recover their account, they may have no choice but to buy it again on a new one, along with everything else they've lost," he said. "A disc-free future will see this become the reality for every new PlayStation game, for gamers across most of Africa."

Sony and PlayStation did not respond to Decrypt's request for comment.

Royal Caribbean's newest megaship is set to make its U.S. debut this weekend.

Passengers will board Legend of the Seas, the cruise line's third Icon Class vessel, at Fort Lauderdale's Port Everglades on Sunday for a pre-inaugural three-night trip to the Bahamas.

The cruise line added the departure ahead of the ship's previously announced Nov. 11 inaugural voyage from the cruise hub, giving travelers an early chance to experience its newest Icon Class ship.

The sailing will include a stop at Royal Caribbean's private island destination, Perfect Day at CocoCay, followed by a day at sea before returning to Fort Lauderdale on Nov. 11.

At 248,633 gross tons, Legend of the Seas is the world's largest cruise ship, sharing the title with its two same-size Icon Class sisters, Icon of the Seas and Star of the Seas. The vessel can accommodate up to 7,600 passengers and approximately 2,350 crew members.

Like its sister ships, Legend of the Seas features eight neighborhoods, seven pools and a lineup of family-friendly attractions, including Category 6, a six-slide water park. The ship also introduces several new experiences, including the Hollywood-themed Hollywoodland Supper Club and additional dining options in the AquaDome.

Related: Royal Caribbean cruise ships from newest to oldest — a complete list

Legend of the Seas debuted in Europe in summer 2026, sailing seven-night Western Mediterranean itineraries from Barcelona and Rome before crossing the Atlantic to begin its Caribbean season.

Following two initial Bahamas sailings, Legend of the Seas will operate six- to eight-night itineraries from Fort Lauderdale to Western and Southern Caribbean destinations — including some places, like Aruba and Curacao, that don't normally see much action from Icon Class ships. Depending on the sailing, it'll also make port calls in Puerto Rico, the Dominican Republic and Mexico. Plus, every sailing will visit Perfect Day at CocoCay.

Rates for six-night trips begin around $1,000 per person, and eight-night trips begin around $1,400. However, prices differ greatly based on your chosen itinerary, room type and dates of travel.

33 Bedroom Plants That Bring a Calming Touch to Your Space

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Do you have one of these in your room yet?

Want to improve your sleep naturally? Try adding a few plants to your bedroom. Beyond offering charm and a cozy atmosphere, plants have been linked to mental and physical benefits, including sleep. Research suggests that being around greenery can lift your mood, ease stress, and promote a greater sense of well-being. Bringing a touch of nature into your sleeping space may be a simple way to add those positive qualities to the room.

Plants may also help calm the mind and move the body from an alert state toward a more restful one, potentially supporting better sleep. A past study linked indoor greenery with more positive emotions and fewer negative ones, which may help you fall asleep faster. The findings point to a broader connection between greenery and well-being, including its possible role in a restful bedroom. It’s easy to see why biophilic design—an approach that evokes nature indoors—has become a favorite among designers.

Ahead, find the best plants for your bedroom. We made sure the options would thrive in all types of lights, but especially in low light, so that whether you’re a beginner or an experienced green thumb, you will find the right verdant friend to grow.

The Aspidistra elatior thrives in low to bright shade, making it perfect for bedrooms that don’t get too much direct light. A fun fact: This plant historically was referred to as the “bar room” plant or “beer plant” because it could survive smoky, low-light conditions in Victorian-era taverns and halls. It is non-toxic to humans, cats, and dogs so if you have pets this is a great option.

Native to the rainforests of Brazil, the Maranta leuconeura is now a common indoor houseplant. During the day, the oval leaves lie flat to soak up the light and at night, the leaves fold upward like praying hands, hence the name.

For a trailing plant that thrives in bright, indirect light get a wax plant. It appreciates higher humidity levels so if you live in an older home with fluctuating humidity, this is the verdant plant baby for you.

For beginner and intermediate plant owners, the nerve plant is a great option because they thrive in most indoor conditions, remain compact, and are safe for pets. They will signal to you what they need so you can water and choose the soil for it accordingly.

Pilea peperomioides, or the Chinese money plant, is a cheerful-looking, easy-to-propagate plant that is incredibly low-maintenance. It's a great plant for a sunny bedroom. All plant parents need to do is place it in bright, indirect sunlight and water only when the top inch of soil feels dry.

Aglaonema tolerates very low light levels, so it's ideal if you don't have large windows in your bedroom. Water it when the top few inches of soil feel dry.

The snake plant doesn't mind low light—and because it's a succulent, it only needs to be watered every week or two.

Purported to bring good luck, the money tree has a nice, modern vibe—plus, it's not fussy and grows quickly. Give it medium light, water it every week or two, and it'll be happy.

The pothos plant tolerates any light level from low to high. (That means it's super-easy to keep alive!) Just don't water it until the top few inches of soil feel dry to the touch—overwatering is the only real way to kill this plant.

Lavender has long been considered beneficial for relaxation and sleep. Although most research has studied the use of lavender essential oils specifically, having a live lavender plant in your bedroom probably can't hurt. It needs bright light, so make sure it's on a sunny windowsill or use a grow light. Water when the top inch or two feels dry to the touch.

This handsome vining plant is a great choice for new plant parents. Its glossy, heart-shaped leaves are beautiful as they spill over the edges of nightstands or bookshelves–and it's tolerant of low light levels. Water when the top few inches of soil feel dry.

This adorable fern is one of the best to raise indoors. Its thick, waxy leaves retain water better than many other ferns that have finer fronds—and it won't leave a mess by dropping foliage every day. Give it bright indirect light and water when the top inch of soil feels dry.

This fun plant looks like an old-fashioned ric rac trim. It will grow like crazy in bright indirect light—and because it's a succulent, it needs watering only every week to 10 days.

This social media darling is striking and easy to grow—it just needs bright indirect light. Water when the top few inches of soil feel dry.

The peace lily will adapt well to low light levels, but it needs bright indirect light to bloom. Water when the top inch or two feel dry.

This cute palm that looks just like a messy ponytail likes bright indirect light. And the bulbous base stores water, so it needs a drink only every few weeks when the soil is totally dry.

This handsome plant boasts a beautiful upright form, so it makes an eye-catching statement in the bedroom. Give it bright indirect light, and water when the top few inches of soil are dry. As an outdoor landscape plant, it will flower, though that's unusual indoors.

If you're looking for low-maintenance houseplants, this is the way to go! It will tolerate low light and is often grown in water. In water, change it weekly; in soil, water so that it stays slightly moist.

This cute tabletop palm will add loads of style to your bedroom. It prefers bright indirect light, but it will tolerate medium light better than many other types of palms. Water when it's slightly dry to the touch.

Burger King's New Advent Calendar Has A Big Surprise Behind Its 12 Doors

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Last year's disappeared in minutes. Consider yourself warned.

Remember when Advent calendars were just little cardboard boxes with a piece of chocolate behind each door? Nowadays, you can find them filled with everything from beauty products to booze—and apparently, miniature fast food restaurants.

Burger King's Advent calendar is returning for a third year, and this time, you can practically open your own franchise.

If you remember last year's edition, it was packed with some pretty unexpected throwbacks, including Cini Mini-inspired candles, a tiny board game, and a JD the Dog plush. Fans ate it up, too, as Burger King said its last two calendars sold out in less than five minutes.

For 2026, the chain has ditched the assortment of random collectibles for something you can actually put together. Joel Yashinsky, Burger King's chief marketing officer, said fans can “build their very own BK restaurant.”

Behind the 12 doors are miniature versions of just about everything you'd find at your local BK: There's a Whopper with its own box, fries, nuggets, a soda fountain, a booth, employees, and customers. And of course, who can forget the essential Christmas tree and a reindeer pairing, too.

The only thing missing is food you can actually eat, although the final compartment does include a $5 digital offer for eligible Royal Perks members.

Now, if you want to get your hands on one, you'll have five chances. Burger King is releasing just 110 calendars each day from November 16 through November 20, with sales starting at 12 p.m. ET exclusively at BKAdventCalendar.com.

Each costs $19.54, a reference to the year Burger King was founded, and orders are limited to one per household, with free standard shipping to the contiguous U.S. and Washington, D.C. If you manage to snag one of the Advent calendars, expect it to ship the week of November 30.

So have yourself a merry little Whopper this Christmas. And I do mean little.

TeamGroup GA Pro 2TB SSD Review: A faltering attempt at a mid-range PCIe 5.0 SSD

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Join for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation plus sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Reviews, benchmarks, and updates on current GPUs.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The TeamGroup GA Pro is a drive that mostly misses. While it has relatively good points at QD8 and acceptable steady-state write speed, overall performance is very low with this inefficient, hot-running drive.

Why you can trust Tom's Hardware Our expert reviewers spend hours testing and comparing products and services so you can choose the best for you. Find out more about how we test.

Every once in a while we get a drive that comes across our desk that stands out, for good or for bad. The TeamGroup GA Pro falls into the latter category, enough so that we re-checked our results and contacted the company. We’ve managed to mostly sort out what’s going on and, while this is not the worst drive around, it has enough problems that we would generally not recommend it except if it's on steep sale.

TeamGroup’s array of storage offerings knows no bounds: if there’s a niche to cover, it makes an attempt. The GA Pro feels like such a try, although we’re not completely sure where it fits into the mix. The company is still working to iron out the bugs, if it can, but the technology is old enough that we wouldn’t hold our breath. On paper, it’s a PCIe 5.0 drive with DRAM that should beat any PCIe 4.0 SSD, but we found it struggles to live up to its own class of drive. Bright points are few and far between, but at least we can say it’s not the worst drive we’ve seen. The edges are just rougher than usual.

The TeamGroup GA Pro is available in 1TB, 2TB, 4TB, and 8TB capacities, although we only have the 2TB showing up at $331.99 at retail right now. The 8TB will probably be hard to find at this point. The drive is rated for up to 10,000 / 8,500 MB/s for sequential reads and writes, with no IOPS rating, although a drive like this can achieve 1 million or more. It has the standard five-year warranty with up to 600 TB of writes per TB of capacity.

TeamGroup has a number of downloads available for this range of drives, which is better than nothing. They do maintain firmware for their drives, and we made sure our test sample had the newest revision, 050g05E7. Also available is the SSD S.M.A.R.T. TOOL, which is their SSD toolbox with basic functions, such as health monitoring. For backup and cloning on Windows, we continue to recommend MultiDrive, which continues to be updated based on user feedback.

The good news is, the GA Pro has a graphene heat-spreading label. The bad news is, it’s insufficient to cool this drive. In most cases, you will want a heatsink on this. You can use a heatsink with the label in place, but you will have better luck if it’s removed. We are fond of graphene labels as they can be surprisingly effective, but it’s just not enough here.

Then we have the label which, with the 3.3V / 2.5A rating, puts the drive at a very optimistic 8.25W. We measured over 12.5W peak in our testing below. We can’t go by reported power states, as these top off at 3.50 W. Since we can’t rely on that, we can’t say for sure what even the expected enter/exit latencies are, relevant given TeamGroup’s claims about the drive, which we’ll get to later. The drive is otherwise double-sided with a controller, two DRAM packages, and four NAND flash packages.

The SSD controller is InnoGrit’s IG5666, the company’s answer to the Phison E26 high-end PCIe 5.0 champion. We have better controllers in that segment now, like the E28 and SMI’s SM2508, but even at the time of release, the IG5666 was not very popular. Both the E26 and IG5666 run very hot due to using an older process node, and newer flash wasn’t available yet to more easily reach the bus speeds needed. The GA Pro is not running full-out but, to be honest, that doesn’t help much. Phison put a lot of work into getting the E26 up to full speed in steps, and even the base 10 GB/s ones run toasty.

Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.

The drive does have the right amount of DDR4, 2GB, for this amount of flash. No problems there. The flash also looks to be YMTC’s 232-layer TLC, which is also perfectly fine. The controller is the weak spot here and, as we’ll find out, its firmware really keeps the GA Pro from reaching its full potential, even with its bandwidth cap.

Epic Games' Quixel Megascans has thousands of 3D models, decals and materials.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

No it's not a flashback to 2024, Epic Games has made its huge Quixel Megascans library free again, and this time it seems it's permanent. Access to the collection of photogrammetry-based 2D and 3D real-world assets was made free for a limited time two years ago to mark the launch of the Fab marketplace, but now it will be free indefinitely.

Quixel features thousands of production-ready items, including 3D models, tileable physically based rendering (PBR) surfaces, vegetation, decals, and custom materials, created by scanning real-world objects, environments. That makes it a massive resource for game developers, 3D artists, VFX artists and those working in architectural visualisation (also see our guides to the best laptops for CAD and the best laptops for 3D modelling).

The full Quixel Megascans library is going free. Fab listings will update between now & Nov 12, so check back if an asset you want isn’t free yet. We’ve updated terms for all Quixel assets so you can train AI models on Megascans & Megaplants. Learn more: https://t.co/jX1C4jRauw https://t.co/nuMDVwvJwcNovember 5, 2026

Megascans currently contains around 23,000 3D scanned assets. Quixel says listings on the Fab marketplace will be updated up until November 12 to make items free. It's also updated the terms for Quixel assets to allow Megascans and Megaplants assets to be used to train AI models.

"Your AI models can now learn from real-world scans to help you generate varied, photorealistic environments for your projects," it says.

Anyone who bought a Megascans or a Quixel Bundle on or after January 1, 2025 should receive a refund to their Epic Account Balance by the end of the year while keeping their licence and getting the new AI rights. The balance can be used across Epic's products, not just on Fab.

Epic has worked to make the new Fab marketplace as easy to use as possible; Channels can be used to discover compatible game engines while a mix of licences ensure you can find the right asset to meet your needs. Fab offers a Creative Commons licence and the Personal or Professional tier of a Standard License. Epic Games press release clarifies: "The Fab Standard License enables you to use the assets, including Megascans, in any game engine or tool you want."

Check out the Megascans library on Faby to see the variety of assets available, from furniture, and architecture to creature, vehicles and materials.

The Best Way to Eat Swiss Fondue? Floating on Lake Brienz

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

I could say that I was surprised to find myself and my family floating along in a red Zodiac on the deepest lake in Switzerland, eating cheese fondue and sipping white wine as the sun set. But really, it was all by design. When I designated myself as chief planner for an autumnal family trip to the Alpine nation with my adult children and their partners, I searched for an activity that would knock their socks off. I found it in Interlaken, with a floating fondue experience. Yes, you read that right.

I love Switzerland, and have visited the land where the hills are alive with the sound of cowbells more than 30 times in my life. And while I’ve done many amazing things in and around the Swiss Alps—skiing, hiking, trottinetting, whittling my own Swiss cow from a block of wood—I knew getting my Gen Z crew excited about family time involved finding something a little more unconventional.

We were going to be staying in the Bernese Oberland, a picture-postcard region with legendary sites like Grindelwald, the Jungfrau and Eiger mountains, and Interlaken. Everyone was up for hiking and taking cable cars to waterfalls; paragliding was sadly vetoed. But a unique culinary experience would surely work, I thought—the way to their hearts is through their stomachs.

Luckily, I had heard about a Cheese Fondue Float experience offered by a company called Outdoor Switzerland a few years ago. Back then, it was raclette—toasted cheese scraped onto boiled potatoes—that served as the dish du jour. But fondue, Switzerland’s national dish, is much more well-known, so the company savvily swapped raclette trays for caquelons (ceramic fondue pots). I found the experience listing on Get Your Guide, and reviews praised the change, highlighting the delicious cheese and wine and the gregarious fondue fun. I confirmed a private booking for the six of us a month before we left home, and kept my fingers crossed for good weather.

I needn’t have worried—my prayers were answered with glorious temperatures and bluebird skies on the day of the tour, a leisurely Sunday in September. A five-minute bus ride from downtown Interlaken brought us to the small village of Bonigen, the launch site located on scenic Lake Brienz. After shaking hands with our guide, a tall, friendly Dutchman named Hild, we donned life jackets, listened to the mandatory safety briefing, and stepped into the boat, alternating sides to keep the weight even. Once comfortably settled around the pretty red-and-white-checkered tablecloth, which was already topped with plates, cups, and two fondue pots, Hild slowly rowed us onto the lake.

An overhead shot of the pretty tablescape and gorgeous lake

Lake Brienz is the deepest lake in the country, and it’s surely one of the most scenic too. Moving out from the shore, we encountered spectacular views of towering mountains, their peaks mirrored in the impossibly blue water. I poured the prepared cheese fondue into the heated fondue pots, and the mixture began bubbling, filling the air with a sharp aroma as it warmed into readiness.

The fondue float experience includes white wine (one 500-ml bottle per person), as well as water and hot tea. We took turns pouring wine into our copper cups as my eldest son took command of the Bluetooth speaker, setting a lively mood with traditional Alpine tunes played on accordions and hackbretts (a kind of dulcimer), mixed with the occasional yodel ditty. At one point, our float’s music and laughter brought friendly waves from passengers on a passing cruise boat, along with toots from the ship’s horn. We whooped and waved in return.

Once the cheese was steaming hot, we dipped and swished bread cubes into the melted goodness, savoring the delicious alchemy of bread and cheese alongside celebratory toasts in honor of our family adventure. As the cheese disappeared, the fondue pot heated the remains into what the Swiss call “la réligieuse”—toasted cheese bits that are scraped from the bottom of the pot. We fought playfully over dibs on the last delicious morsels—trust me, they’re good to the last perfectly browned crumb.

Raising a glass to a unique family adventure

The entire experience lasted about an hour and a half, and during that time, the six of us managed to drink all six wine bottles on board. None of us are big drinkers, but the beauty of the surroundings inspired the good times to flow, and, well, so did the wine.

Photos and videos captured our cheesy adventure, but the memory of enjoying such a unique culinary and cultural experience with my favorite people in the world will be forever burned into my mind. You can have a fondue anywhere, but while floating in one of the most iconic locations in one of the most beautiful countries on earth? Even my discerning Gen Z travel companions agreed: “That’s extra.”

The Cheese Fondue Float experience on Lake Brienz in Interlaken is available to book on the Outdoor Switzerland website, as well as on GetYourGuide and Viator. It is offered between September and April, weather permitting. If booking in the colder months, bundle up with coats, beanies, and gloves, as you’ll be out on the water for up to an hour and a half. Private charters are available for groups up to eight people; kids eight years and up are welcome. More of a dessert person? Outdoor Switzerland also offers a Swiss Chocolate Fondue experience. Get cozy under blankets, sip hot tea, and dip marshmallows and fruit into creamy melted Swiss chocolate while a qualified guide rows you merrily along the beautiful Brienzersee.

As the most discerning, up-to-the-minute voice in all things travel, Condé Nast Traveler is the global citizen’s bible and muse, offering both inspiration and vital intel. We understand that time is the greatest luxury, which is why Condé Nast Traveler mines its network of experts and influencers so that you never waste a meal, a drink, or a hotel stay wherever you are in the world.

Nov. 7, 1882: A Great Comet transforms astrophotography

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

In early September 1882, a comet was sighted from the Cape of Good Hope in South Africa. The comet brightened over the following weeks, reaching a magnitude of -2 on the 15th; at perihelion two days later, it was bright enough to be visible during the day. But at the time, David Gill, the director of the Cape Observatory, was busy observing minor planets Victoria and Sappho. It wasn’t until Oct. 12 that, his minor planet observations completed, he turned his attention to the comet and began photographing it.

Gill captured images of the Great Comet of 1882 on Oct. 12, 20, and 21, and Nov. 7, 13, and 14. The Nov. 7 shot was the one he would become famous for, though: It was not the first photograph of a comet, but rather would signal a change in astronomers’ uses of astrophotography. The photo was a 100-minute exposure, resulting an image that included stars to 10th magnitude and the comet’s 18-degree tail. Spurred by the large number of stars captured in his photo, Gill saw the potential for using astrophotography for star mapping and began the Cape Photographic Durchmusterung. (Durchmusterung means “close examination” or “scrutiny.”) The project would catalog nearly a half-million southern stars. Gill shared the successes of the program internationally, prompting the assembly of the world’s first major astronomical conference. As a result, astronomers worldwide began to move away from traditional, manual star mapping and towards more efficient and comprehensive photographic techniques.

Elisa Neckar is senior production editor of Astronomy magazine, and the editor of the Today in the History of Astronomy feature on Astronomy.com.
